## Exact-file-content inference and resumed coding acceptance · 2026-10-28

- A bounded isolated Qwen3-VL 4B coding attempt created `isWithinRange.mjs` and a test fixture, but did not pass. Its first test used Jest-style globals under `node:test`; a later process call used a URI-encoded checkout path containing `%20` and failed with `spawn node ENOENT`. The model's `task_complete` then correctly rejected missing evidence but exposed a separate false inferred requirement: because the objective said “Run exactly node --test isWithinRange.test.mjs” after naming `isWithinRange.mjs`, Xyst required the source file to equal the literal text `node --test isWithinRange`. The session was stopped as a non-pass. Only the two disposable files from this attempt were moved from the checkout root into its isolated temp workspace; no source file was changed by the model.
- Fixed `inferExactFileContentRequirements` to consider an `exactly` or `single line` directive only when no sentence boundary separates it from the named output file. Added a regression using the observed objective, successful process evidence and exact reads of both outputs. The new regression and existing exact-output completion case passed (2/2); the full runtime suite passed 171 tests with 2 host-specific skips, and workspace typechecks plus `git diff --check` passed.
- PR #25 (`0820028`) adds the traceability audit and workflow-dispatch history. It passed all eight duplicated Windows/Linux hosted checks and merged as `c51b529233710a6a2cbbc5ac7de2e5e8ae5d0d3a`. Post-merge Cross-platform CI `40629168196` and Desktop packages `40629168174` both passed on that SHA. The model-backed range task remains a failure/non-pass; this deterministic runtime fix does not convert it into an acceptance pass.

## Configuration import/export credential redaction · 2026-10-28

- The two-brief audit found that `ConfigStore.export()` and `import()` filtered only top-level setting names. A benign setting name could contain nested `Authorization` or `X-Api-Key` fields, or a credential-bearing string, and pass through the portable settings file.
- Export and import now omit the entire setting when its key or any nested object key is credential-like, or when a string contains a bearer/basic credential, URL user-info, or credential query parameter. Omitting the whole setting avoids producing a partial integration configuration. Updated `docs/configuration.md` and the A4 traceability row.
- Focused redaction tests passed 2/2. Full `npm test` passed: runtime 172 passed, 2 host-specific skips, 0 failures; CLI 23/23; release verification 5/5. `npm run typecheck` passed for runtime, CLI, and desktop. `git diff --check` passed with only existing LF/CRLF normalization notices. This verifies the redaction logic, not exhaustive secret detection for arbitrary user-defined values.

## CLI config-import filesystem policy · 2026-10-28

- The configuration audit found `xyst config import <file>` read the user-specified path without calling `AccessController.assertPath`, unlike other file and integration configuration readers. Added active-profile read-path enforcement and a 1 MiB import-file limit before parsing.
- The isolated CLI regression imports a settings file from an allowed read root, rejects a file outside that root, rejects an oversized file, and confirms the allowed setting persisted. Focused CLI test file passed 2/2; full `npm test` passed with runtime 172 passed/2 host skips, CLI 24/24, release verification 5/5; workspace typechecks and `git diff --check` passed. This validates the CLI command on this Windows host, not every filesystem adapter.

## Workspace-mutation retry during document-template acceptance · 2026-10-28

- A bounded live run of the `document-spreadsheet` mission template with already-loaded `qwen/qwen3-vl-4b` created the requested PDF during its planning task, then the artifact task tried to create the same path. The first create correctly failed because the destination existed; after `file_delete` succeeded, a repeated create was incorrectly replayed as the same failure and the model entered a repeated-call loop. The mission ended `failed`. This is a non-pass for C5 live template acceptance; it did not verify the requested content or visual inspection.
- The failure exposed an idempotency gap for workspace mutations. `run_process` already accounted for intervening workspace changes, but `file_create` and related workspace actions did not. Those actions now include the latest other completed workspace mutation in their request key, allowing a retry after a successful delete while still replaying an unchanged successful action. A focused regression confirms the initial `EEXIST`, successful delete, successful retry, and replay of the unchanged result.
- Focused regression passed (1/1), runtime typecheck passed, and the full runtime suite passed (173 passed, 2 host-specific skips, 0 failures). This deterministic recovery fix does not convert the failed live template attempt into a pass; C5 remains partial pending end-to-end acceptance.

## Office preview preserves source readback evidence · 2026-10-28

- A post-PR #35 live mission retry was not a pass: LM Studio returned HTTP 500 for `/v1/chat/completions`. Inspection of the saved tool history also exposed a deterministic evidence bug: `office_preview` reads its `path` input and writes only `output_path`, but stale-evidence detection treated the source path as modified. This incorrectly invalidated the preceding `office_read` of the PDF.
- Stale-evidence path matching now treats `office_preview` as a read of `path` and a write to `output_path`. A regression performs `office_read`, `office_preview`, confirms image pixels remain attached to the next model request, and completes with both required evidence entries; task completion is verified.
- Focused regression passed 1/1. Full `npm test` passed: runtime 175 passed/2 host-specific skips, CLI 24/24, release verification 5/5. `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. This closes a deterministic verification defect; the failed LM Studio retry remains a non-pass, and the full document mission acceptance remains open.

All eight Windows/Linux PR/push checks passed, as did post-merge CI [40653301808](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/40653301808) and Desktop packages [40653301812](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/40653301812).
- Post-PR #38 isolated mission `22b59990-28c1-45e8-9e38-bdddfb5824e4`, run `269eac74-725a-486a-96d9-b630daa28459`, completed its plan, create and verify tasks with persisted `task_complete verified=true`. The verifier ran on `qwen/qwen3-vl-4b`, used only `office_read`/`office_preview`, and its session `894346bd-aa35-43c5-ba46-a516577c7672` persisted a multimodal `image_url` containing the 1,224 × 1,584 PNG (`workshop-brief-preview.png`, SHA-256 `f17ff3b904761858c1d9c8ccb7bec7a3c4e4433c5b51c8b6f17db34c3d6edf98`) and a matching PDF source hash `dea4d63f76612715d12998582a7a32db987831b51a77e8c6687ea2cb0c7675e8`.
- Independent pixel inspection and packaged offline Tesseract OCR confirmed one visible title, the required date and audience, three numbered items, readable page layout, and whole-word wrapping. The page uses generous blank space below the content. This is a bounded live pass for one one-page PDF workflow; it does not demonstrate broad model reliability, other formats, resolutions, templates or platforms. C5 remains partial; E5 is verified for the bounded fixture and this isolated live PDF.

## Project-scoped configuration during mission planning · 2026-10-28

- The A4 audit found `MissionPlanner.plan` used project scope for network policy only, while resolving `model.default`, `model.autoSelect`, `orchestration.delegation.enabled`, and `orchestration.maxConcurrency` globally. It also omitted `projectId` from the generated mission definition, so task execution could not inherit project-scoped settings. The CLI had no project option for planning and checked global network access before the planner could apply a project override.
- Mission planning now resolves all of those settings in the supplied project context and preserves the project ID in its draft. The CLI supports `mission plan <objective> --project <project-id>` and delegates network authorization to the planner, where project policy still cannot widen a restricted access profile. Configuration documentation and A4 traceability describe the behavior.
- Local verification: focused planner regressions passed; full runtime suite passed (178 passed, 2 host-specific skips), CLI suite passed (26/26), release checks passed (5/5), workspace typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. Regressions set conflicting global/project model, auto-selection, delegation, concurrency, and network values, then confirm project values determine the planning request and returned mission scope while the restricted access profile still denies network.

## Supported scopes for documented settings · 2026-10-28

- The A4 audit found the desktop scoped editor offered all five scopes for every documented key even where runtime consumers only read the global or project/mission value. A user could save an override that appeared active but had no effect.
- Added a shared supported-scope policy used by runtime writes, imports, effective reads, portable exports, and the desktop editor. Documented global-only keys reject scoped writes/imports; mission policy and resource settings permit project/mission overrides. Legacy rows at unsupported scopes are ignored and omitted from exports so they cannot silently shadow valid settings. Unknown custom keys retain generic scope support.
- Full local `npm test` passed (runtime 178 passed, 2 host-specific skips; CLI 26/26; release verification 5/5), `npm run typecheck` passed across runtime, CLI, and desktop, and `git diff --check` passed. Regression coverage includes rejection, editor scope mapping, safe handling of a legacy unsupported row, and atomic import rejection. Broader optional-setting behavior and import/export coverage remain open under A4.

## Global permission gates for scoped mission settings · 2026-10-28

- The A3/A4 audit found that mission-scoped GitHub `enabled`, `writeEnabled`, and `mergeEnabled` values could override the matching global `false` value through normal scope precedence, contrary to the documented requirement that mission grants also need global/runtime permission. A scoped `autonomy.externalActionApproval=false` could likewise mask a global `true` and skip an intended approval prompt.
- Agent tool construction now requires both global and effective scoped GitHub access/write/merge switches. External-action approval is required if either the global or effective scoped value enables it, so narrower configuration may add confirmation but cannot remove a global requirement. Mission definitions retain their ability to narrow grants or enable them after global policy permits them.
- A focused runtime regression verified globally disabled GitHub access exposes no GitHub tools, a mission write grant cannot execute when global writes are off, mission merge authorization cannot bypass the global merge switch, and a mission approval override cannot skip a globally required confirmation. Focused test passed 1/1; full local `npm test` passed (runtime 179 passed, 2 host-specific skips; CLI 26/26; release verification 5/5); workspace typecheck passed. A4/A3 broad settings and hostile-content audits remain open.

## Concurrent mission cancellation across inference and child processes · 2026-10-28

- Added an integration regression using the real `AgentRunner` and `PersistentMissionRunner` with two independent mission tasks. One task waits on an in-flight model request while the other runs a child process scheduled to write a delayed marker. Cancelling the mission aborts both, leaves both task rows pending for resumption, and the child creates no marker after its scheduled write time.
- Focused test passed 1/1. This is one bounded concurrent cancellation case; sustained task batches, concurrent tool calls within tasks, repeated pause/resume, and broader delegated graphs remain unverified under A5.

## Repeated mission pause and resume · 2026-10-28

- Added a runtime integration regression that pauses one persistent mission during inference three times, resumes the saved task after each interruption, checks the task remains pending with attempt counts preserved, then completes the fourth execution.
- Focused test passed 1/1. This covers three sequential pause/resume cycles for one task; larger task graphs, concurrent tool batches, and broader delegated graphs remain unverified under A5.

## Mission concurrency stress with sixteen independent tasks · 2026-10-28

- Added a deterministic `PersistentMissionRunner` integration regression with 16 independent tasks in distinct workspaces and `maxConcurrency: 4`. Each fake agent call has a short varied delay to exercise backfill. The test verifies all 16 task IDs start once, peak activity reaches exactly four without exceeding the limit, and every persisted task row completes with one attempt.

## Exact output text followed by an explicitly named tool · 2026-10-28

- An isolated live mission showed that an objective such as `alpha.txt containing exactly alpha-ok using file_create` could be misread as requiring the literal contents `alpha-ok using file_create`. That live attempt was stopped and is **not** an acceptance pass.
- Updated completion-evidence inference to recognize a following `using`/`via`/`through` clause only when it names an available Xyst tool. A focused agent regression creates and reads `alpha.txt` with the exact contents `alpha-ok` and completes only from persisted `file_create` plus exact-path `file_read` evidence.
- The focused regression passed. Full `npm test` passed: runtime 183 passed / 2 host-specific skips, CLI 26/26, release verification 5/5. Workspace `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. This closes one deterministic completion-inference edge case; broader model reliability remains open.
- A second isolated three-task Qwen3.5 9B mission retry (`23eef67b-fe8d-4e5e-9b58-2539716f8c9a`) also did **not** pass. The alpha task wrote and read `alpha.txt`, then the model attempted the beta task from within alpha's four-turn budget; the runner failed alpha at the turn limit, and the dependent verifier never ran. This is recorded as failed delegated-model evidence, not as an acceptance pass.

- Persisted sessions: alpha `35f57676-9ba8-426d-9e50-6030628b5477`, beta `def39715-7a3b-45dc-a65a-2d1aab6239bc`, verifier `560dea3b-0db3-451d-8a2d-1281fdf4c7bd`. ## Atomic memory index invalidation · 2026-10-28

- Memory edits now update content and remove that memory's cached semantic embeddings in a single SQLite transaction, so interruption cannot leave an old vector attached to new text.
- The focused memory regression passes 1/1 and verifies edited content is absent from semantic retrieval until re-indexed, deleting the final memory cascades to its embedding, and semantic search no longer returns the deleted entry. Full `npm test` passed (runtime 183 passed / 2 host-specific skips, CLI 26/26, release checks 5/5), `npm run typecheck` passed, and `git diff --check` passed. Hosted checks have not yet run for this change.
- This closes deterministic edit/delete propagation for the tested profile. Broader retrieval quality and larger-profile deletion behavior remain open; E8 stays partial.

## Version-one SQLite profile upgrade · 2026-10-28

- Built a clean legacy v1 SQLite profile with the original session/message/tool, mission, agent, job, artifact, usage, and settings schema plus a saved user message. Opening it with the current runtime applied migrations 2 through 15, preserved the exact historical message, and returned `PRAGMA integrity_check = ok`.
- Focused migration regression passed 1/1. Full `npm test` passed (runtime 185 passed / 2 host-specific skips, CLI 26/26, release checks 5/5), workspace `npm run typecheck` passed, and `git diff --check` passed. This verifies a clean version-one upgrade path only; other starting versions, large production-sized databases, and crash interruption remain open under E7.

## SQLite migration rollback and retry · 2026-10-28

- A new regression forced migration 15 to fail after its `session_compactions` table was created but before its index was added. SQLite rolled back the table creation, left `schema_migrations` at 14, preserved the saved session message, and passed `PRAGMA integrity_check`; after removing the deliberate conflicting index, reopening the runtime applied migration 15 successfully and preserved the message. `XystStore` now closes its database handle if initialization or a migration throws.
- The focused migration test passed 1/1; full Windows runtime suite passed 191 tests with 2 host-specific skips and 0 failures. Workspace `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. This verifies one bounded failed-statement rollback/retry case, not process-kill interruption or every historical starting version; E7 remains partial.

## SQLite migration process-termination recovery · 2026-10-28

- A separate Node process was terminated after migration 15 executed its `session_compactions` table and index DDL inside the SQLite transaction, but before the migration-version row was inserted or the transaction committed. Reopening the database showed schema version 14, no `session_compactions` table, the original saved message, and `PRAGMA integrity_check = ok`; retrying through `XystStore` applied version 15 and preserved the message and integrity.
- The focused process-crash test passed 1/1 on Windows. Cross-platform hosted checks will verify the same case on Windows and Linux. Other historical starting versions, production-sized databases, and other interruption boundaries remain open under E7.
## Mission scheduler missed-run policy · 2026-10-28

- Added deterministic restart coverage for two due schedules: `run_once` executes the mission once with the exact persisted `next_run_at` timestamp, then advances the next run; `skip` emits no mission run for stale work.
- The focused behavior passed as part of the complete Windows local runtime suite (186 passed, 2 host-specific skips), along with CLI 26/26, release verification 5/5, workspace `npm run typecheck`, and `git diff --check`. C4 remains partial; this does not establish long-running multi-runner or bare-metal service behavior.

## Concurrent mission runner lease isolation · 2026-10-28

- Added a regression with two independent `PersistentMissionRunner` instances and separate SQLite connections to one profile. While the first holds the mission lease inside its agent call, the second sees the live lease immediately, rejects without invoking the agent, and creates no second run; the lease owner then finishes successfully.
- Focused test passed 1/1; full local verification passed (runtime 187 passed / 2 host-specific skips, CLI 26/26, release checks 5/5), workspace `npm run typecheck`, and `git diff --check`. This tests separate runtime/store instances in one process, not process-kill or long-duration multi-runner stress; C4 remains partial pending that broader coverage.

## Cross-process mission runner lease isolation · 2026-10-28

- Added a regression that launches a real second Node process using the compiled runtime and a separate SQLite connection to the same mission profile. While the child process holds the mission lease during agent execution, the parent runner is rejected before its agent is called; the child completes and the database contains exactly one mission run.
- Focused cross-process test passed 1/1. The 1.5-second contention case does not establish long-duration stress, crash recovery at every lease boundary, or multi-host behavior; C4 remains partial.

## Mission runner lease fencing · 2026-10-28

- Added a deterministic stale-owner regression: while a runner agent is active, the persisted lease is replaced by a live successor owner. The stale runner observes failed renewal, aborts its agent signal, and cannot rewrite the successor's mission/run/task state or emit a final event.
- Focused test: `mission runner stops without finalizing state after another owner takes its lease` passed locally on Windows. This is deterministic fencing evidence, not long-duration cross-process stress; C4 remains partial.

## Cross-process takeover of an expired mission lease · 2026-10-28

- Added a second process-level regression: the original process holds mission work, its persisted lease expires, and a separate Node process resumes that same run. The old process aborts and returns without finalizing the successor's active state; then the successor completes the run, and persisted run/task/mission state is completed exactly once.
- Focused takeover test passed 1/1 on Windows. This proves one bounded expired-lease handoff; long-duration contention/restart stress remains open under C4.

## Windows mission service lifecycle command-boundary regression · 2026-10-28

- Added a Windows Task Scheduler lifecycle regression that runs the CLI's injectable PowerShell boundary for install, status, and uninstall. It decodes the actual UTF-16LE `-EncodedCommand`, checks quoted executable/CLI arguments, webhook options, at-logon trigger, limited interactive principal, single-instance/restart settings, status JSON parsing, and stop/unregister commands.
- `npm run test --workspace @xyst/cli` passed 27/27 and workspace `npm run typecheck` passed on Windows. A separate `scripts/smoke-windows-mission-service.mjs` now installs the daemon through the real CLI on the disposable hosted Windows package runner, waits for Task Scheduler to report `Running`, then uninstalls and confirms the task is absent. It refuses to replace a preexisting task and cleans up after failures; the PR #55 hosted Windows smoke later passed and the PR merged. No Windows Task Scheduler task has been registered on the user's PC. This covers one ephemeral runner session, not logoff/reboot recovery or long-term service behavior.

## Desktop appearance preference behavior · 2026-10-28

- Corrected preference selectors to match the kebab-case `data-*` attributes generated from `document.documentElement.dataset` for reduced motion, high contrast, larger text, decorative effects, and robot presentation. The prior camel-case selectors did not match those attributes. Larger-text mode now applies Chromium page zoom at 1.15× so fixed-pixel renderer text scales too; high-contrast mode also brightens muted text while increasing panel borders.
- A disposable headless Electron run connected to the isolated Vite renderer and checked computed behavior in the actual Chromium page: a navigation target grew from 39 px to 44.84375 px, page zoom was 1.15, the border color changed from `#233432` to `#79988b`, muted text changed from `rgb(113, 129, 125)` to `rgb(214, 226, 218)`, and spinner duration fell from `0.8s` to `1e-05s` under reduced motion. Typecheck, desktop production build, both packaged GUI smoke-script syntax checks, and `git diff --check` passed locally. The Windows and Linux package smokes now assert those appearance behaviors; hosted Windows/Linux clean-install checks passed on PR #58.
- These checks establish tested renderer behavior, not native screen-reader or physical display accessibility; F2 remains partial.
## LM Studio callable inference ID preflight · 2026-10-28

- A live CLI image run exposed a mismatch: Xyst selected loaded instance `qwen/qwen3-vl-4b:2`, but LM Studio returned HTTP 400 `model_not_found` from `/v1/chat/completions`. The error was retained as a failed attempt, not an acceptance pass. A later read-only inventory no longer listed that instance, so the exact live state could not be reproduced after the change.
- `LMStudioProvider.resolveInferenceModelId` now checks IDs against OpenAI-compatible `GET /v1/models` before a loaded instance ID reaches agent inference. An advertised base key can replace a missing instance ID only when exactly one instance is loaded. Explicit instance selections and ambiguous or absent IDs fail before any prompt is sent. The remote fallback path, which sends its configured base ID, does not incur this loaded-instance check.
- Two focused regressions cover exact advertised IDs, the single-instance base fallback, uncallable/explicit IDs, and the `AgentRunner` request path. All passed. Full Windows runtime suite passed 193 tests, 2 host-specific skips, 0 failures. Workspace `npm run typecheck` passed for runtime, CLI, and desktop; runtime and CLI builds passed.
- After the fix, a fresh isolated Qwen3.5 9B CLI image attachment run returned exactly `VISION-731` using the already-advertised model ID. Persisted session `62c341dc-51f0-405b-8894-f2f2e35e175b` is `completed`, contains one actual `image/png` data URL in the user message and the exact assistant response, and its temporary profile passes SQLite integrity check. No model was loaded or unloaded.

- Added an AgentRunner-level regression with `memory.semantic.enabled=true` and `memory.embeddingModel=manual-embedder`. The provider inventory also offers a different loaded embedder, so the test verifies semantic `memory_search` uses the explicitly configured model rather than automatic selection.

## Hostile web content cannot authorize an external write · 2026-10-28

- Added an AgentRunner integration regression that fetches a local mocked public HTML response containing a prompt-injection instruction. The deterministic model receives the returned `untrusted-web-content`, then attempts a GitHub issue comment even though the user's objective asks only for a summary and explicitly forbids changes.
- With global GitHub read/write enabled and `autonomy.externalActionApproval=true`, the run fails with `EXTERNAL_ACTION_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`; the comment provider is never called, the web fetch remains completed, and the GitHub write intent is persisted as failed. This verifies enforcement at the external-action boundary; it does not prove model resistance to prompt injection across all inputs, platforms, or profiles.
- Focused test passed 1/1 on Windows. Full workspace `npm test` passed (runtime 192 passed / 2 host-specific skips, CLI 27/27, release checks 5/5); workspace typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. A3 remains partial for additional desktop, CLI, mission, and hostile-content paths.

## GitHub mutation approval and recovery coverage · 2026-10-28

- A source audit compared mutating GitHub tools and browser actions against AgentRunner's external-action set and SQLite migration classifications. It found issue-label add/replace/remove, pull request update, and `browser_close_tab` omitted. The GitHub tools had the global write switch, and tab close required external-actions access, but all five could bypass interactive approval, durable external intent, idempotency, and uncertain-result recovery.
- Added all five actions to AgentRunner's external-action classification and migration 16. The migration assigns historical records to an execution scope and marks interrupted `running` actions `uncertain`, forcing explicit reconciliation instead of replay.
- AgentRunner regressions verify each GitHub provider remains uncalled without user approval and that closing a tab likewise never executes without approval; a migration regression verifies all five historical interrupted actions are quarantined and SQLite remains healthy. The hostile `web_fetch`-to-GitHub authority regression remains in place.
- Focused security/migration tests, including browser tab close, passed 4/4. Full workspace `npm test` passed (runtime 195 passed / 2 host-specific skips, CLI 27/27, release checks 5/5); workspace typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. This closes the identified classification omissions but does not prove every integration workflow or malicious-input path.
## MCP image sampling input · 2026-10-28

- MCP sampling previously rejected image blocks even though its request schema accepts them. The runtime now converts bounded base64 image blocks into LM Studio's OpenAI-compatible image input while preserving accompanying text. It allows PNG, JPEG, WebP and GIF, caps each image at 5 MiB and total image bytes per request at 10 MiB, and rejects unsupported audio and image MIME types.
- Deterministic regressions prove a text-plus-image request reaches the multimodal provider with the image bytes intact and returns sampled text; image-bearing tool results reach the model as well. Tests reject unsupported MIME, malformed base64, >5 MiB individual images and >10 MiB total request data. The live harness scripts/verify-mcp-image-sampling-live.ts refuses to load a model and requires an already-loaded vision model. On 2026-10-28, qwen/qwen3.5-9b at context 16,384 answered exactly “red” for the ground-truth PNG both as user image content and as an image-bearing request-scoped tool result. See the machine proof and fixture. This is one model/fixture and direct local sampling-handler coverage, not external MCP transport or broad model reliability.
## GitHub API cancellation preserves abort outcomes · 2026-10-28

- The GitHub provider was wrapping fetch aborts as ordinary connection failures in single requests, pagination, App identity lookup, workflow-log retrieval, and archive downloads. It now rethrows the original error when the supplied signal is aborted; non-cancellation network errors still receive GitHub-specific context. No requests are retried, so writes are not replayed after cancellation.
- Expanded the live loopback regression to abort both a single repository request and pagination against a server that never responds; both surface `AbortError`. Focused regression passed 1/1. ## GitHub REST rate-limit response guidance · 2026-10-28

- The GitHub provider previously identified only one primary-limit case (HTTP 403 with zero remaining) and omitted the reset deadline. It now explains primary reset time from `X-RateLimit-Reset`, secondary throttling via `Retry-After` (including HTTP-date values), and 403/429 fallback guidance of at least 60 seconds. Requests are never automatically slept/replayed, avoiding accidental repetition of writes.
- Deterministic regressions cover primary 403 reset, secondary 403 with `Retry-After`, and 429 without a retry header; each rejects with actionable guidance and confirms exactly one request. Focused regression passed 1/1. Full Windows `npm test` passed: runtime 192 passed / 2 host-specific skips, CLI 27/27, release checks 5/5. Workspace `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed.
- Provider behavior follows the current GitHub REST rate-limit guidance: https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api. This does not claim a live throttling event; D2 remains partial pending live rate-limit and other organization/platform cases.
## GitHub rejected-credential and private-404 guidance · 2026-10-28

- A successful `xyst github auth status` identity was cached for five minutes. If a later GitHub API call returned HTTP 401, the cached identity could still make a subsequent status call look authenticated. HTTP 401 now invalidates both the cached identity and GitHub App installation token, and explains how to check authentication and replace/verify credentials. It does not automatically replay the request. HTTP 404 errors now explain that GitHub may hide inaccessible private resources behind a not-found response and advise checking authentication and repository permissions.
- Deterministic loopback/provider tests verify a previously successful identity is requested again after a 401, recovery guidance appears without including the fake token, and a 404 is not described as definitive proof that a private resource is absent. Both focused regressions passed. Full Windows `npm test` passed: runtime 193 passed / 2 host-specific skips, CLI 27/27, release verification 5/5. Workspace typechecks and `git diff --check` passed.

## Concurrent workspace patches reject stale writers · 2026-10-28

- `patchText` serializes edits to the same normalized path within a runtime process and takes a `proper-lockfile` lock for cooperating processes. Each writer re-reads and validates its expected SHA-256 after acquiring both locks, so stale Xyst writers cannot overwrite the winning atomic rename. Temporary files are cleaned if creation or replacement fails. `proper-lockfile` uses an atomically created lock directory and refreshes its mtime; recovery from a crashed owner is bounded by its stale timeout.
- The same-process regression starts two patch calls with one source hash: exactly one succeeds, the other gets the stale-file error, and the final file matches the winner's hash. A separate child-process regression holds the shared lock while changing the target; the patch waits for release, then rejects its stale hash. Both focused regressions passed on Windows. After the cross-process extension, the full Windows runtime suite passed 211, skipped 2 host-specific checks, failed 0; full workspace typecheck passed; `npm audit --omit=dev` reported zero vulnerabilities. Require the new hosted Windows/Linux checks before claiming cross-platform acceptance.
- These locks coordinate Xyst patch calls and other writers that honor the same lock. An external editor that ignores the lock can still race the hash-check/rename window; network filesystems and non-cooperating external editors remain unverified.

## POSIX process-tree cancellation escalates for stubborn descendants · 2026-10-28

- POSIX process cancellation now sends `SIGTERM` to the detached process group, checks whether any group members remain during a 500 ms grace period, and sends `SIGKILL` to the group if needed. This handles a parent that exits promptly while a grandchild ignores `SIGTERM`; checking only the direct child would miss that case. Windows keeps using `taskkill /T /F`.
- A Windows full runtime suite passed 211, skipped 3 environment-specific tests, failed 0; the abort test passed. The POSIX-only regression is intentionally skipped on Windows. WSL Ubuntu Node 24 ran the compiled current `platform.js` against a live process tree: the parent exited on `SIGTERM`, the ready-marked grandchild ignored it, and the regression verified the grandchild was gone after escalation (1/1). Runtime build, typecheck, and `git diff --check` passed.
- Hosted Linux CI is still required for the new POSIX test inside the complete runtime suite. Interactive PTY cancellation and additional Unix variants remain open.

## PTY cancellation terminates stubborn descendants · 2026-10-28

- PTY process jobs now use the shared process-tree termination path: Windows runs `taskkill /T /F`; POSIX signals the PTY process group with `SIGTERM` and escalates to `SIGKILL` after 500 ms if members remain. This closes the gap where terminal cancellation killed only the PTY leader and could leave its shell descendants running.
- The new Windows CLI integration regression starts a native ConPTY process with a descendant that ignores `SIGTERM`, cancels the tree, and verifies the descendant cannot perform a delayed write. It passed locally. Existing native PTY input/output/resize regression also passed.
- Full local verification: runtime 211 passed, 3 environment-specific skips, 0 failures; CLI 29 passed; release-verification scripts 5 passed; full workspace typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. At source commit `b4488965945ea02f566f2ee53688b47466c978fd`, hosted Windows and Ubuntu full CI suites plus Windows NSIS and Linux AppImage package workflows all passed in push and PR runs (`40751708869`, `40751708957`, `40751719331`, `40751719765`). The live PTY descendant regression therefore passed on both hosted operating systems. Additional Unix variants remain open.

## Desktop development launcher uses Vite's actual port · 2026-10-28

- The old desktop `dev` script waited on `127.0.0.1:5173` and Electron defaulted to that URL while Vite could silently move to another port. This machine already had ports 5173 through 5178 occupied, so Electron could attach to a stale renderer.
- Replaced the fixed-port `concurrently`/`wait-on` command with `apps/desktop/scripts/dev.cjs`. It reserves an available loopback port, starts Vite with strict binding on that port, and passes the exact resolved URL to Electron as `VITE_DEV_SERVER_URL`; closing Electron shuts down the paired Vite server.
- `node --check apps/desktop/scripts/dev.cjs`, desktop TypeScript typecheck, and `git diff --check` passed. `npm run desktop:dev` completed the runtime/CLI/desktop builds and started Vite on `http://127.0.0.1:54553/` with the prior 5173–5178 range occupied; an Electron Xyst Control Room window appeared in the window inventory. The same renderer is visible in the browser preview, where it correctly has no Electron runtime bridge. The fullscreen foreground app prevented a reliable native-window inspection, so this verifies launch wiring and renderer visibility, not the Electron runtime UI state.

## Windows process-timeout stall reproduced and bounded · 2026-10-29

- On the pre-fix PR #72 source, the normal `a process timeout after writing a file still requires external-action reconciliation` regression passed in 1.44 seconds. Re-ran it with an isolated .NET `taskkill.exe` shim that waited eight seconds before killing the child; the same test passed in 9.37 seconds without manual process termination. Source inspection confirmed Windows `killProcessTree` waited without a deadline for the helper's `close` event, while `runProcess` waited for the child `close` event after timeout. This reproduces the long wait mechanism; it does not establish the exact cause of the earlier approximately 57-second incident.
- Updated PR #72 `platform.ts`: resolve `taskkill.exe` from `SystemRoot\System32`, bound the helper wait to five seconds, kill the direct child if helper startup/finalization fails, and reject promptly with explicit uncertainty about descendants. `runProcess` races helper failure against child close so a surviving descendant cannot hold the caller indefinitely. The POSIX process-group escalation behavior remains unchanged. Added Windows regressions for unavailable `taskkill` and empty PATH.
- With an isolated eight-second helper shim after the fix, `runProcess` returned after 5.35 seconds with `Process termination could not be verified; descendants may still be running`; the helper was stopped and the direct child was killed by the fallback, without manual intervention. The ordinary timeout, missing-helper, empty-PATH, and abort regressions passed 4/4 on Windows, and runtime typecheck passed. The first typecheck exposed a missing boolean return in the POSIX ESRCH branch; corrected and reran checks successfully.
- These are local Windows results only. Exact-head Windows/Linux hosted workflows must pass after the documentation/source commit. PR #72 remains OPEN/DRAFT; no merge performed, and authorization beyond #58–#60 remains absent.

## PR #72 mission completion response consistency fix · 2026-10-29

## Process-tree termination escalation · 2026-10-28

- POSIX process cancellation sends `SIGTERM` to the detached process group and now schedules a referenced `SIGKILL` after a one-second grace period if the group still exists, even if the original leader has already closed. The referenced timer keeps a standalone CLI alive long enough to apply the fallback. Windows retains its `taskkill /T /F` behavior.
- A regression starts a standalone Xyst child process, waits until its grandchild confirms that its SIGTERM-ignoring handler is installed, aborts the process group, then waits past the grandchild's delayed-write deadline and confirms no marker file was created. The focused regression passed 1/1 on Windows. Full local Windows runtime suite passed 194 tests, 2 host-specific skips, 0 failures; runtime build and workspace `npm run typecheck` passed; `git diff --check` passed.
- This is representative process-tree coverage. A descendant that creates a new session/process group can still escape; PTY control/resize and broader packaged terminal acceptance remain open under E2.

## Windows process-tree timeout failure reporting · 2026-10-29

- A previous full Windows runtime suite stalled for about 57 seconds in a process-timeout test and continued only after its child was manually terminated. That run is not independent cancellation evidence. Ten subsequent focused timeout runs completed without intervention in 1.41–1.51 seconds; the stall did not recur, but these focused runs do not reproduce full-suite load or establish sustained reliability.
- A standalone Windows spawn with an empty `PATH` confirmed that bare `taskkill` lookup can fail to start with `ENOENT` while the child remains alive. `killTree` now resolves `taskkill.exe` through `%SystemRoot%\System32` (or `%WINDIR%\System32`) so cancellation does not depend on `PATH`, bounds the `/T /F` wait to five seconds, and, on start failure, nonzero exit, or timeout, attempts to kill the direct child. If process-tree termination cannot be verified, `runProcess` rejects with an explicit uncertainty error rather than waiting indefinitely. The direct-child fallback cannot guarantee descendant termination.
- Focused Windows regressions passed for: tree termination with an empty `PATH` (no delayed marker and the expected timeout); failure to start `taskkill` (explicit uncertainty and no delayed direct-child write); and persisted `AgentRunner` timeout reconciliation (`uncertain` tool-call status). The correctly built full Windows runtime suite passed 201 tests, skipped 2 host-specific cases, and failed 0; runtime build, runtime typecheck, and `git diff --check` passed. An earlier attempt to force a missing system root into the child environment caused Node startup failure and two test failures; the test now isolates the simulated taskkill path from the child environment. That run is not counted as a pass. Earlier test attempts before the required runtime build and a manually interrupted broad run are also not counted.
- PR #81 code head `b2d718e0dcd7087d9c6efcd7a9da04a8e673f7f9`, stacked on #79, passed all eight hosted Windows/Linux verification and package checks, including Windows clean-install renderer and live mission-service lifecycle smoke tests. PR #81 remains open and unmerged. PR #79 and #80 each had eight passing checks on their inspected heads and remain unmerged. Merge authorization still covers only #58–#60.

## External workspace edits and command replay · 2026-10-29

- In the JSONL session, the later `node --test` tool message repeated the earlier 6/7 output without a new `tool_calls` execution row. The test fixture had been corrected outside Xyst. `AgentRunner` hashes commands using a workspace revision derived from recorded Xyst mutations, so external changes do not invalidate the replay. This is strong evidence that stale output was replayed; the independent 7/7 run remains separate from the agent result.

The existing reconciliation API applies only to uncertain external actions. A user-authorized fresh execution must preserve durable intent and must not silently repeat arbitrary side effects; this capability and live acceptance remain open. C2 is therefore partial.

## Ordinary command output evidence enforcement · 2026-10-29

- Reproduced a false-positive completion: objective `Run a command and verify stdout exactly equals "EXPECTED".` ran a successful process that printed `WRONG`, then the runtime accepted `task_complete` using that process result.
- Added machine-checkable inference for explicitly quoted stdout assertions phrased as `stdout exactly equals/matches "..."` or `stdout includes/contains "..."`. These add a run_process criterion requiring exit code 0 plus exact or substring stdout matching, enforced during candidate mapping and final criterion verification. The requirement remains active if `run_process` is not allowed, so unrelated evidence cannot satisfy it. Unstructured semantic criteria remain outside this narrow parser and still need broader evidence design.
- Deterministic regressions confirm the wrong output is rejected, a corrected command satisfies both exact and inclusion assertions, and a restricted tool set without `run_process` cannot complete the stdout criterion. Focused tests passed. Final Windows `npm test`: runtime 246 tests (243 passed, 3 host-specific skips), CLI 31/31, release verification 5/5. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run desktop:build`, and `git diff --check` passed.

All cover Ubuntu/Windows verification and Linux/Windows packaging. PR #82 remains OPEN/DRAFT and unmerged. The overall project remains incomplete.

## Completion evidence revalidation for rendered previews · 2026-10-29

- `office_preview` completion evidence now requires recorded source-document and rendered-PNG SHA-256 values. At completion, the runtime reauthorizes both paths for reading and hashes current bytes. A missing hash or access/filesystem error fails closed and asks for a fresh render; a changed source or image rejects stale evidence and tells the model to render and inspect again.
- Added an end-to-end deterministic AgentRunner regression using a generated PDF. It externally replaces the source after preview, observes task completion rejection, renders the new PDF, externally changes the PNG, observes a second rejection, then renders and verifies against a fresh preview. This specifically prevents claiming visual inspection of content that has changed since the model received the pixels.
- Windows root `npm test` passed runtime 248 (245 passed, 3 host skips), CLI 31/31, release verification 5/5. Typecheck, desktop production build, and `git diff --check` passed. No Linux or hosted exact-head checks cover this current local source/doc diff yet.

## Completion-time refresh for web fetch and search evidence · 2026-10-29

- Completion now re-executes a cited `web_fetch` or `web_search` using its original input and current access/network configuration. It compares the redacted semantic result, ignoring only the volatile `retrieved_at` field; all other fields must match. Invalid timestamps or saved results, unavailable tools, refresh errors, changed content/results, and output at the 30,000-character comparison limit fail closed. A selected web result is refreshed once even when it supports multiple completion criteria. Truncated or unsuccessful `web_fetch` evidence is rejected before refresh and cannot establish completion.
- Added AgentRunner regressions for a page edited outside Xyst, stable page content with a changed retrieval timestamp, and changed search results outside Xyst. Existing truncated-fetch scenarios now assert that only a cited complete page is refreshed once. The three new cases and two existing truncated-fetch cases passed focused (5/5).
- The first full-suite run exposed two old request-count assertions that assumed no completion-time refresh. It also exposed repeated refresh for one evidence record selected against multiple criteria; the implementation now memoizes one refresh promise per evidence ID. After those corrections, Windows `npm test` passed runtime 255 (252 passed, 3 host-specific skips, 0 failed), CLI 31/31, release verification 5/5. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run desktop:build`, and `git diff --check` passed.
- This source/test/docs change is local and uncommitted on `codex/integrate-pr72-through-command-rerun`, after published PR #82 head `79f9111e080058bd345186ea1ad2a29babba241c`. No hosted checks cover it. PR #82 remains OPEN/DRAFT and unmerged. ## Completion evidence revalidation for file searches · 2026-10-29

- Completion now re-executes a selected `file_search` through the registered access-checked tool using its saved input. The redacted result is compared with the persisted result; changed matches, malformed saved inputs/results, unavailable tools, access/filesystem errors, or output at the 30,000-character cap fail closed and require a fresh, narrower search. This detects out-of-band edits that change matching lines before `task_complete`.
- Added an AgentRunner regression that changes a matching line outside Xyst between the search and completion. Stale completion is rejected; a fresh search supports verified completion. The focused regression passed in the Windows root suite.
- Windows `npm test` passed runtime 257 (254 passed, 3 host-specific skips, 0 failed), CLI 31/31, release verification 5/5. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run desktop:build`, and `git diff --check` passed.
- This closes one bounded `file_search` freshness gap under C2. The comparison does not establish completeness of results beyond the tool's bounded file/match limits, and broader stale evidence, representative sustained acceptance, platform/accessibility coverage, the historical CLI recovery-selector discrepancy, the original Windows timeout stall, and signed production release/update hosting remain open. These local source and documentation edits have no exact-head hosted checks yet; PR #82 remains OPEN/DRAFT and unmerged.
